# SC22 evidence: inventory of the Chief of Staff's rules

Round 3, sentence level. Ids (`C-nnn`) are drafts that child 26 replaces. The new files are in `skills/chantier/cards-next/` (`chief-of-staff.md`, `topics/`, `topics.json`).

## How the sources were walked

- Sources, `skills/chantier/references/` at the worktree's base: chief-of-staff (317 lines), chief-of-staff-any (202 lines), chief-of-staff-batch-start (248 lines), chief-of-staff-steering (346 lines), chief-of-staff-merge (222 lines), chief-of-staff-batch-end (168 lines), chief-of-staff-direct (223 lines), chief-of-staff-grill (477 lines), chief-of-staff-release (150 lines), chief-of-staff-writer (21 lines), session-start (182 lines), session-start-chief-of-staff (153 lines), titles (41 lines), ticket-summary-example (45 lines). Headings, blank lines and fenced command lines that no sentence carries get no row of their own.
- One row per rule sentence: a sentence ends at a full stop outside a code span; a sentence of more than 190 characters is cut again at a top-level semicolon. A row's `source` is the line, or the two lines, where its sentence sits at the base; no row spans a block. `behaviour` is the sentence itself, cut at 240 characters; `trigger` is the nearest heading above it. The walk was run by a script (`work/coder3/gen3.py` and `emit3.py` in the CR folder, on the part-1 splitter `work/coder/split.py`); a row's home is the best match between its sentence and the new card, the floor and the topics, and a hand table (`OV` in `emit3.py`) settles the rows the match could not place. Every row was then read through the lists at the end of this file.
- Kinds (study-fable section 3): a protects the user or the repository; b inferable by a capable model; c incident or retro patch; d host trial and "unproved" bookkeeping; e state or procedure the CLI prints just in time, or a topic carries; f duplicate. A kind a row has a home in the card, the floor or a CLI refusal, never a topic alone (F5), and no protecting row is inferable. A procedure carried by a topic only is kind e (or c), not a; where such a row applies a protecting rule, its `new home` names the topic and, after `the rule it applies:`, the card line or floor rule that holds the rule itself.
- New home vocabulary: `card <file>:<n>`, `floor <n>` (`floor.md`), `refusal <command, message start>`, `next:` line, `topic <name>`, `host row`, `inferable`, `duplicate`.
- Checks: `grep -c '<phrase>' <card>` names a phrase to find in the card; `help <topic>` means `$CHANTIER help <topic>` prints it (`load_topics` loads `topics.json`); `none (<why>)` where the floor owns the rule, or it is dropped.
- Roles: the topics of this family carry `chief-of-staff` (some also `griller`, the grill topics). The role column of a row is `chief-of-staff`, or `chief-of-staff, griller` for a sentence of a file both read.
- Source check: for each row the lines named in `source` were read at the base (`git show HEAD:skills/chantier/references/<file>.md`) and each must hold a word of the row's behaviour: 0 rows failed.

## Changed behaviours and rails

Rows whose behaviour changes on purpose in this round (answer 8), for child 25 to reconcile. This round does not edit `evals/SCORECARD.md`.

- New, with no row at the base: "settles alone" item 3 (a refused `gh pr ready <n>` or `gh pr edit <n> --base <branch>` on a PR the record holds for a CR of this batch: run once alone, note it, say it in the report) and item 4 (a chantier subcommand the host refused in a chain: run again alone). They are in `chief-of-staff.md:8-9` and `help refused-forge-chain`; the old cards name no such route, and a refused `gh` command was a question to the user.
- Unchanged: the rebase route (item 2, `chief-of-staff.md:7`; rows of `chief-of-staff-steering.md` on the refused rebase).
- The refused force push (item 1) keeps the old judgement and the plain `git push --force-with-lease` in the route form; what changes is the wording of the ladder: the Chief of Staff settles alone and says it in the report's Notes, and asks one question only when the host refuses its own run (`chief-of-staff.md:10`).
- Narrower rails in `evals/SCORECARD.md`: J11 describes a ladder ending in "one question to the user and one push on the yes" and names no rebase, no `gh` route and no chained-subcommand route; J22 lists the steering session's exceptions as the cleanup of merged CRs' worktrees, the force push on a Driver's `push` line and the fast-forward of the main checkout, and does not list the refused rebase (`git -C <worktree> rebase`), nor `gh pr ready` and `gh pr edit --base`, which the card now has the session run. Both rails then read narrower than the card (J11 and J22).
- Open question carried: sc22-Q1, whether a non-CR branch is never force-pushed, nor the default branch, even on a yes (the card says never, a yes included, for the default branch and its force push; any other branch: once, on the yes). And sc22-Q2, whether direct mode's `brief foreman --epoch <n>` needs a CLI change (the card writes it as is; none is made).
